<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\portfolio\instruments.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/instruments.py

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum
from typing import Final

# ============================================================
# INSTRUMENT (ABI-STABLE IDENTIFIERS)
# ============================================================


class Instrument(IntEnum):
    """
    Instrument = dispatch identifier for evaluation units.

    This includes:
    - tradable contracts (spot, forwards, options,bonds, swaps, etc)
    - stochastic loss processes (credit JTD)

    Behavior is defined by EvaluationTopology, NOT by instrument name.
    """

    # --- Market Risk ---
    SPOT = 0

    VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE = 1
    VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE = 2

    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE = 3
    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE = 4


NUM_INSTRUMENTS: Final[int] = len(Instrument)


INSTRUMENT_NAMES = frozenset(inst.name for inst in Instrument)

# ============================================================
# IDS FOR DISPATCH (SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH)
# ============================================================

SPOT_ID: Final[int] = Instrument.SPOT.value
VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE_ID: Final[int] = Instrument.VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE.value
VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE_ID: Final[int] = Instrument.VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE.value
VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE_ID: Final[int] = (
    Instrument.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE.value
)
VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE_ID: Final[int] = (
    Instrument.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE.value
)


# ============================================================
# EVALUATION TOPOLOGY (PRIMARY DISPATCH AXIS)
# ============================================================


class EvaluationTopology(IntEnum):
    """
    Defines HOW an object is evaluated.
    This is the primary driver of execution strategy.
    """

    MTM = 0  # direct evaluation (spot, forwards)
    PATH_DEPENDENT = 1  # depends on full path
    OPTIMAL_STOPPING = 2  # requires backward induction


# ============================================================
# OPTIONAL: INSTRUMENT FAMILY (NON-DISPATCH)
# ============================================================


class InstrumentFamily(IntEnum):
    SPOT = 0
    FORWARD = 1
    OPTION = 2
    CREDIT = 3


# ============================================================
# CORE REGISTRIES (SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH)
# ============================================================


INSTRUMENT_TO_TOPOLOGY: Final[dict[Instrument, EvaluationTopology]] = {
    # --- MTM ---
    Instrument.SPOT: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
    Instrument.VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
    Instrument.VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
    # --- Options ---
    Instrument.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
    Instrument.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
}


INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY: Final[dict[Instrument, InstrumentFamily]] = {
    Instrument.SPOT: InstrumentFamily.SPOT,
    Instrument.VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE: InstrumentFamily.FORWARD,
    Instrument.VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE: InstrumentFamily.FORWARD,
    Instrument.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE: InstrumentFamily.OPTION,
    Instrument.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE: InstrumentFamily.OPTION,
}


# ============================================================
# VALIDATION (HARD FAIL ON DRIFT)
# ============================================================


_ALL = frozenset(Instrument)

if _ALL - frozenset(INSTRUMENT_TO_TOPOLOGY):
    raise RuntimeError("Missing topology mappings")

if _ALL - frozenset(INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY):
    raise RuntimeError("Missing family mappings")


# ============================================================
# FAST DISPATCH ARRAYS (CRITICAL FOR PERFORMANCE)
# ============================================================


# Dense arrays for O(1) lookup without dicts
INSTRUMENT_TO_TOPOLOGY_ARRAY: Final[list[int]] = [
    INSTRUMENT_TO_TOPOLOGY[Instrument(i)] for i in range(0, NUM_INSTRUMENTS)
]

INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY_ARRAY: Final[list[int]] = [
    INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY[Instrument(i)] for i in range(0, NUM_INSTRUMENTS)
]


# ============================================================
# EDGE (STRING) REGISTRIES — ONLY FOR I/O
# ============================================================


INSTRUMENT_NAME_TO_INSTRUMENT_REGISTRY: Final[dict[str, Instrument]] = {
    inst.name: inst for inst in Instrument
}

INSTRUMENT_NAME_TO_ID_REGISTRY: Final[dict[str, int]] = {
    inst.name: inst.value for inst in Instrument
}

INSTRUMENT_ID_TO_NAME_REGISTRY: Final[list[str]] = [
    Instrument(i).name for i in range(0, NUM_INSTRUMENTS)
]


# ============================================================
# INSTRUMENT FAMILY TO INSTRUMENT NAME REGISTRIES
# ============================================================


# Family → list of instrument names (all instruments in a family)
FAMILY_TO_INSTRUMENT_NAMES: Final[dict[InstrumentFamily, list[str]]] = {
    family: [inst.name for inst, fam in INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY.items() if fam == family]
    for family in InstrumentFamily
}

# Family → list of instrument IDs
FAMILY_TO_INSTRUMENT_IDS: Final[dict[InstrumentFamily, list[int]]] = {
    family: [inst.value for inst, fam in INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY.items() if fam == family]
    for family in InstrumentFamily
}

# Family name (string) → list of instrument names
FAMILY_NAME_TO_INSTRUMENT_NAMES: Final[dict[str, list[str]]] = {
    family.name: [
        inst.name for inst, fam in INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY.items() if fam == family
    ]
    for family in InstrumentFamily
}


if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Instument.
    print("\nInstruments:")
    for inst in Instrument:
        print(f"  {inst.name} (ID={inst.value})")

    # Sanity check: print all registries
    print("\nINSTRUMENT_TO_TOPOLOGY:")
    for inst, topo in INSTRUMENT_TO_TOPOLOGY.items():
        print(f"  {inst.name} → {topo.name}")

    print("\nINSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY:")
    for inst, fam in INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY.items():
        print(f"  {inst.name} → {fam.name}")

    print("\nFAMILY_TO_INSTRUMENT_NAMES:")
    for fam, insts in FAMILY_TO_INSTRUMENT_NAMES.items():
        print(f"  {fam.name} → {insts}")

    print("\n --- END: ALL CHECKS PASSED --- \n")
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\portfolio\validation.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/validation.py
"""
Pre-compile validation of `IngestBatch` global-ID references against the
`UixMapper` universes.

Runs ONCE between universe discovery (`build_uix_mapper`) and block
compilation. Catches any global ID or ordinal that the upstream resolver
emitted but the universe-builder didn't see — i.e. a referential-integrity
bug somewhere in the ingest pipeline. By doing this before compile, the
hot-path `map_*_to_uix` calls can stay branch-light and trust their input.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Iterable, Sequence

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.portfolio.engine import UixMapper
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.batch import IngestBatch


def validate_ingest_batches_against_uix_universe(
    *,
    ingest_batches: Sequence[IngestBatch],
    uix_mapper: UixMapper,
) -> None:
    """
    Validate that every non-sentinel ID and ordinal referenced by the
    ingest batches is present in the corresponding `UixMapper` universe.

    Raises `ValueError` with the first batch of offending values it finds,
    grouped by universe.
    """
    # --- MARKET IDs -------------------------------------------------------
    _check_arrays_against_universe(
        arrays=(
            arr
            for b in ingest_batches
            for arr in (
                b.underlying_ids,
                b.volatility_ids,
                b.fx_u2s_ids,
                b.fx_k2s_ids,
            )
        ),
        universe=uix_mapper.market_id_universe,
        label="market IDs",
    )

    # --- DISCOUNT CURVE IDs -----------------------------------------------
    _check_arrays_against_universe(
        arrays=(
            arr
            for b in ingest_batches
            for arr in (
                b.under_discount_curve_ids,
                b.strike_discount_curve_ids,
            )
        ),
        universe=uix_mapper.discount_curve_id_universe,
        label="discount-curve IDs",
    )

    # --- DATES ------------------------------------------------------------
    _check_arrays_against_universe(
        arrays=(
            arr
            for b in ingest_batches
            for arr in (b.settle_ord, b.delivery_ord, b.expiry_ord)
        ),
        universe=uix_mapper.date_ord32_universe,
        label="ordinal dates",
    )


def _check_arrays_against_universe(
    *,
    arrays: Iterable[npt.NDArray[np.int32]],
    universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    label: str,
) -> None:
    """
    For every input array, assert that every value >= 0 also appears in
    `universe`. Vectorized: one `searchsorted` per input array, no Python
    loop over rows.
    """
    for arr in arrays:
        populated = arr[arr >= 0]
        if populated.size == 0:
            continue

        idx = np.searchsorted(universe, populated)
        in_bounds = idx < universe.size

        matches = np.zeros(populated.shape[0], dtype=bool)
        matches[in_bounds] = universe[idx[in_bounds]] == populated[in_bounds]

        if not matches.all():
            missing = np.unique(populated[~matches])
            raise ValueError(
                f"Unknown {label} encountered during UIX validation: {missing}"
            )
```

```python
from pandas import DataFrame
from pandas.api.types import is_string_dtype


def validate_string_cols(df: DataFrame, cols: tuple[str, ...] | list[str]) -> None:
    for col in cols:
        if col not in df.columns:
            continue

        s = df[col]

        if not is_string_dtype(s):
            raise ValueError(f"Column '{col}' must be of string dtype.")

        non_na = s.dropna()
        if non_na.empty:
            continue

        # Length
        max_len = non_na.str.len().max()
        if max_len > 255:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Column '{col}' contains strings longer than 255 characters "
                f"(max={max_len})."
            )

        # Canonical format
        pattern = r"^[A-Z0-9]+(?:_[A-Z0-9]+)*(?:\[(?:[A-Z]+[+-]\d+|(?:ON|TN|SN|\d+[DWMY]))\])?$"
        if not non_na.str.match(pattern).all():
            raise ValueError(
                f"Column '{col}' contains non-canonical strings. "
                f"Expected format: UPPERCASE tokens separated by underscores."
            )
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\utils\dates.py">
```python
# riskquant/utils/dates.py
from __future__ import annotations

import datetime as dt
from collections.abc import Iterable
from typing import Final

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.constants import DATE_EPOCH

DATE_EPOCH_AS_DATETIME64D: Final[np.datetime64] = np.datetime64(
    DATE_EPOCH.isoformat(),
    "D",
)


def coerce_as_of_date_to_pd_timestamp(
    as_of_date: pd.Timestamp | dt.date | pd.Timestamp | str,
) -> pd.Timestamp:
    """
    Accepts: pd.Timestamp | datetime.datetime | datetime.date | str
    Returns: pd.Timestamp (tz-naive)
    """
    # If str, validate format.
    if isinstance(as_of_date, str):
        assert_date_str_is_iso_format(as_of_date)

    # As pd.Timestamp
    ts = pd.Timestamp(as_of_date)

    # If caller passed a date (no time), treat it as end-of-day inclusive
    # so 2026-01-02 includes all times on 2026-01-02.
    if isinstance(as_of_date, dt.date) and not isinstance(as_of_date, dt.datetime):
        ts = ts + pd.Timedelta(days=1) - pd.Timedelta(nanoseconds=1)  # type: ignore[operator]  # mypy doesn't know Timestamp supports this math

    # Normalize tz handling (if any). Adjust policy as you like.
    if ts.tzinfo is not None:
        ts = ts.tz_convert(None)

    return ts


def is_date_str_valid(date_str: str) -> bool:
    """Checks if date_str which must have form %Y-%m-%d is a valid calendar date."""
    if not isinstance(date_str, str):
        return False
    if len(date_str) != 10 or date_str[4] != "-" or date_str[7] != "-":
        return False
    if not date_str.replace("-", "").isdigit():
        return False

    try:
        pd.to_datetime(date_str, format="%Y-%m-%d", errors="raise")
    except ValueError:
        return False

    return True


def assert_date_str_is_iso_format(date_str: str) -> None:
    """Assert that date_str is in ISO format 'YYYY-MM-DD'."""
    if not is_date_str_valid(date_str):
        raise ValueError(f"Date string '{date_str}' is not in 'YYYY-MM-DD' format.")


def date_str_to_date(date_str: str) -> dt.date:
    """Convert a date string in 'YYYY-MM-DD' format to a datetime.date object."""
    assert_date_str_is_iso_format(date_str)
    return pd.to_datetime(date_str, format="%Y-%m-%d", errors="raise").date()


def date_to_ord32(d: dt.date) -> np.int32:
    return np.int32((d - DATE_EPOCH).days)


def ordinal32_to_date(days: int) -> dt.date:
    return DATE_EPOCH + pd.Timedelta(days=int(days))  # or datetime.timedelta


def datetime64D_to_ord32(dts: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Convert numpy datetime64[D] array -> int32 days since DATE_EPOCH.
    Returns a view/copy depending on input; no pandas involvement.
    """
    dts = np.asarray(dts, dtype="datetime64[D]")
    return (dts - DATE_EPOCH_AS_DATETIME64D).astype(np.int32, copy=False)


def ordinal32_to_datetime64D(days: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Convert int32 days since DATE_EPOCH -> numpy datetime64[D] array.
    """
    days = np.asarray(days, dtype=np.int32)
    # Cast to timedelta64[D] then add epoch
    return DATE_EPOCH_AS_DATETIME64D + days.astype("timedelta64[D]", copy=False)


def ym_str_to_int_tuple(ym: str) -> tuple[int, int]:
    """Convert 'YYYYMM' -> (YYYY, MM)."""
    if len(ym) != 6 or not ym.isdigit():
        raise ValueError(f"Invalid YM string: {ym}")
    y, m = int(ym[:4]), int(ym[4:6])
    if not 1 <= m <= 12:
        raise ValueError(f"Invalid YM string: {ym}")
    return y, m


def ymd_str_to_date(ymd: str) -> dt.date:
    """Convert 'YYYYMMDD' -> datetime.date."""
    if len(ymd) != 8 or not ymd.isdigit():
        raise ValueError(f"Invalid YMD string: {ymd}")
    return dt.date(int(ymd[:4]), int(ymd[4:6]), int(ymd[6:8]))


def month_diff(
    *,
    anchor_ym: tuple[int, int],
    target_ym: tuple[int, int],
) -> int:
    """Difference in months: target - anchor."""
    ay, am = anchor_ym
    ty, tm = target_ym
    return (ty - ay) * 12 + (tm - am)


def next_half_year_boundary(
    t0: dt.date,
    ym_boundaries: Iterable[str] = ("0630", "1231"),
) -> dt.date:
    """
    Return the first boundary date >= t0 among {YYYY0630, YYYY1231} (or configured boundaries).
    If none remain in the year, return the first boundary in the next year.
    """
    ym_bounds = list(ym_boundaries)
    if not ym_bounds:
        raise ValueError("No half-year boundaries provided.")

    year = t0.year
    candidates = []
    for b in ym_bounds:
        if len(b) != 4 or not b.isdigit():
            raise ValueError(f"Invalid year-month boundary 'MMDD': {b}")
        m = int(b[:2])
        d = int(b[2:])
        candidates.append(dt.date(year, m, d))

    for d in sorted(candidates):
        if d >= t0:
            return d

    # none left this year -> first boundary next year (sorted)
    year += 1
    b0 = sorted(ym_bounds)[0]
    return dt.date(year, int(b0[:2]), int(b0[2:]))


def half_year_index(d: dt.date) -> tuple[int, int]:
    """
    Map boundary date to a half-year index:
      YYYY-06-30 -> (YYYY, 0)
      YYYY-12-31 -> (YYYY, 1)
    """
    if (d.month, d.day) == (6, 30):
        return d.year, 0
    if (d.month, d.day) == (12, 31):
        return d.year, 1
    raise ValueError(f"Not a recognized half-year boundary: {d.isoformat()}")


def half_year_diff(
    *,
    anchor: dt.date,
    target: dt.date,
) -> int:
    """Difference in half-years: target - anchor, where each boundary step counts as 1."""
    ay, ah = half_year_index(anchor)
    ty, th = half_year_index(target)
    return (ty - ay) * 2 + (th - ah)


def get_previous_business_date_as_iso_str(
    *,
    lag_in_bus_days: int = 0,
    holiday_dates: list[str] | tuple[str, ...] | None = None,
    time_str_to_append_to_date: str | None = None,
) -> str:
    # Ensure holidays is a list of strings.
    if holiday_dates is None:
        holiday_dates = []
    if isinstance(holiday_dates, tuple):
        holiday_dates = list(holiday_dates)
    if not isinstance(holiday_dates, list):
        raise TypeError("holiday_dates must be a list!")

    # Create a custom business day with the holidays.
    custom_business_day = pd.offsets.CustomBusinessDay(holidays=holiday_dates)

    # Start from today and apply the lag and one additional business day.
    start_date = dt.datetime.today().date()
    adjusted_date = start_date - custom_business_day * (lag_in_bus_days + 1)
    date_as_str = adjusted_date.strftime("%Y-%m-%d")

    # Append the time string if provided, and return.
    if time_str_to_append_to_date is not None:
        return date_as_str + f" {time_str_to_append_to_date}"
    return date_as_str


def get_previous_business_date_as_date(
    *,
    lag_in_bus_days: int = 0,
    holiday_dates: list[str] | tuple[str, ...] | None = None,
    time_str_to_append_to_date: str | None = None,
) -> dt.date:
    # Get as string.
    date_string = get_previous_business_date_as_iso_str(
        lag_in_bus_days=lag_in_bus_days,
        holiday_dates=holiday_dates,
        time_str_to_append_to_date=time_str_to_append_to_date,
    )

    # To date.
    return dt.date.fromisoformat(date_string)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/utils/printer.py
from __future__ import annotations

import math
from collections.abc import Iterable, Mapping
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Any

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

# ==========================================
# DEFAULT STYLING & ANSI COLORS
# ==========================================
ANSI_CYAN = "\033[96m"
ANSI_GREEN = "\033[92m"
ANSI_FOREST_GREEN = "\033[38;5;28m"  # A rich, medium-dark green (#008700)
ANSI_DEEP_GREEN = "\033[38;5;22m"  # A very dark, subtle green (#005f00)
ANSI_YELLOW = "\033[93m"
ANSI_RED = "\033[91m"
ANSI_MAGENTA = "\033[95m"
ANSI_BOLD = "\033[1m"
ANSI_RESET = "\033[0m"
ANSI_BLUE = "\033[34m"  # Standard Dark/Deep Blue
ANSI_BRIGHT_BLUE = (
    "\033[94m"  # High-intensity Blue (matches the brightness of your other variables)
)
ANSI_MIDNIGHT_BLUE = "\033[38;5;17m"  # Deepest dark blue (#00005f)
ANSI_NAVY_BLUE = "\033[38;5;18m"  # Slightly brighter navy (#000087)

ANSI_BLACK = "\033[30m"
ANSI_BRIGHT_BLACK = "\033[90m"  # Dark gray, great for subdued metadata
ANSI_BLACK_BG = "\033[40m"


DEFAULT_TITLE_COLOR = ANSI_BLUE


def print_header(
    *,
    title: str,
    step_delta: int | None = None,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """
    Prints a stylized, rounded Unicode box containing the title.
    Acts as the master title renderer for all other print functions.
    """
    if step_delta is not None:
        title_ = f"STEP N - {abs(step_delta):1.0f}: {title}"
    else:
        title_ = title

    c = color or DEFAULT_TITLE_COLOR

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    width = len(title_) + 2
    # print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}╭{'─' * width}╮{ANSI_RESET}")
    # print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}│ {title_} │{ANSI_RESET}")
    # print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}╰{'─' * width}╯{ANSI_RESET}")
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}┏{'━' * width}┓{ANSI_RESET}")
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}┃ {title_} ┃{ANSI_RESET}")
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}┗{'━' * width}┛{ANSI_RESET}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_subheader(
    *,
    title: str,
    step_delta: int | None = None,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """
    Prints a stylized, rounded Unicode box containing the title.
    Acts as the master title renderer for all other print functions.
    """
    if step_delta is not None:
        title_ = f"STEP N - {abs(step_delta):1.0f}: {title}"
    else:
        title_ = title

    c = color or DEFAULT_TITLE_COLOR

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    width = len(title_) + 2
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD}  {title_}  {ANSI_RESET}")
    print(f"{c}{ANSI_BOLD} {'─' * width} {ANSI_RESET}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_text(
    text: str,
    *,
    width: int = 70,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
) -> None:
    import textwrap

    if add_space_before:
        print()
    print(textwrap.fill(text, width=width))
    if add_space_after:
        print()


def as_list_str(x: Iterable) -> str:
    # Convert an iterable of items to a nicely formatted string with each
    # item on its own line, prefixed by "- ".
    items = (str(i) for i in x)  # generator avoids extra allocations
    return "- " + ",\n- ".join(items) + "."


def print_as_list_str(x: Iterable) -> None:
    print(as_list_str(x))


def print_wrapped_text_box(
    text: str,
    *,
    width: int = 70,
    padding: int = 1,
    corner_char: str = "+",
    top_border: str = "-",
    bottom_border: str = "-",
    horiz_border: str = "|",
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """Word‑wrap `text` to `width` characters and display it in an ASCII box."""
    import textwrap

    lines = textwrap.wrap(text, width=width)
    if not lines:
        lines = [""]
    max_len = max(len(line) for line in lines)

    inner_width = max_len + padding * 2
    horiz = corner_char + top_border * inner_width + corner_char

    c = color or ""
    reset = ANSI_RESET if color else ""
    bold = ANSI_BOLD if color else ""

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    print(f"{c}{bold}{horiz}{reset}")
    for line in lines:
        print(
            f"{c}{bold}{horiz_border}{reset}"
            + " " * padding
            + line.ljust(max_len)
            + " " * padding
            + f"{c}{bold}{horiz_border}{reset}"
        )
    print(f"{c}{bold}{corner_char + bottom_border * inner_width + corner_char}{reset}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_success_message(
    message: str | None = None,
    *,
    width: int = 70,
    padding: int = 2,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    uppercase: bool = True,
    color: str | None = ANSI_GREEN,
) -> None:
    """
    Print a success message inside a wrapped, rounded ANSI box (matching print_header).
    """
    import textwrap

    s = datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
    if message:
        s += f" | {message}"
    else:
        s += " | SUCCESS! CODE RAN SUCCESSFULLY!"

    text = s.upper() if uppercase else s.capitalize()

    # Handle text wrapping
    lines = textwrap.wrap(text, width=width)
    if not lines:
        lines = [""]
    max_len = max(len(line) for line in lines)
    inner_width = max_len + padding * 2

    c = color or ""
    reset = ANSI_RESET if color else ""
    bold = ANSI_BOLD if color else ""

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    # Top border
    print(f"{c}{bold}╭{'─' * inner_width}╮{reset}")

    # Body (Color applied to the entire line)
    for line in lines:
        print(
            f"{c}{bold}│"
            + " " * padding
            + line.ljust(max_len)
            + " " * padding
            + f"│{reset}"
        )

    # Bottom border
    print(f"{c}{bold}╰{'─' * inner_width}╯{reset}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_calling_file(
    *,
    width: int = 70,
    padding: int = 2,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    color: str | None = ANSI_BLACK,
) -> None:
    """
    Print the name of the Python file that called this function
    inside a wrapped, rounded ANSI box (matching print_header).
    """
    import inspect
    import os
    import textwrap

    frame = inspect.currentframe()
    try:
        caller_frame = frame.f_back if frame else None
        filename = caller_frame.f_code.co_filename if caller_frame else "<unknown>"
        filename = os.path.basename(filename)
    finally:
        # Explicitly delete frame references to avoid reference cycles
        del frame

    dt_str = datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")
    text = f"{dt_str} | RUNNING FILE: {filename}"

    # Handle text wrapping
    lines = textwrap.wrap(text, width=width)
    if not lines:
        lines = [""]
    max_len = max(len(line) for line in lines)
    inner_width = max_len + padding * 2

    c = color or ""
    reset = ANSI_RESET if color else ""
    bold = ANSI_BOLD if color else ""

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    # Top border
    print(f"{c}{bold}╭{'─' * inner_width}╮{reset}")

    # Body (Color applied to the entire line)
    for line in lines:
        print(
            f"{c}{bold}│"
            + " " * padding
            + line.ljust(max_len)
            + " " * padding
            + f"│{reset}"
        )

    # Bottom border
    print(f"{c}{bold}╰{'─' * inner_width}╯{reset}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_dict(
    d: dict[Any, Any | np.integer | np.floating]
    | Mapping[Any, Any | np.integer | np.floating],
    *,
    title: str | None = None,
    indent: int = 0,
    indent_step: int = 4,
    sort_keys: bool = False,
    limit: int | None = None,
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    title_color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """Pretty-print a dictionary with aligned keys and nested support."""
    items = sorted(d.items()) if sort_keys else list(d.items())
    if limit is not None:
        items = items[:limit]

    try:
        max_key_len = max(len(str(k)) for k in d)
    except ValueError:
        max_key_len = 0

    pad = " " * indent

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    if title:
        print_header(
            title=f"Dictionary: {title}",
            color=title_color,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=False,
        )

    print(pad + "{")
    for key, value in items:
        key_str = str(key)
        key_pad = key_str.ljust(max_key_len)

        if isinstance(value, (Mapping, dict)):
            print(pad + " " * indent_step + f"{key_pad}:")
            print_dict(
                d=value,
                indent=indent + indent_step * 2,
                indent_step=indent_step,
                sort_keys=sort_keys,
                limit=None,
                add_space_before=False,
                add_space_after=False,
            )
        else:
            print(pad + " " * indent_step + f"{key_pad}: {value}")

    print(pad + "}")

    if add_space_after:
        print()


class _FloatFormatter:
    __slots__ = ("_fmt", "_zero_tol")

    def __init__(self, spec: str, zero_tol: float = 0.0) -> None:
        if "_" in spec:
            clean_spec = spec.replace("_", ",")
            self._fmt = lambda x, s=clean_spec: f"{x:{s}}".replace(",", "_")
        else:
            self._fmt = lambda x, s=spec: f"{x:{s}}"
        self._zero_tol = float(zero_tol)

    def __call__(self, x: float) -> str:
        if x is None or (isinstance(x, float) and math.isnan(x)):
            # Handle NaN alignment for Numpy formats
            try:
                width = int(
                    self._fmt.__defaults__[0]
                    .split(".")[0]
                    .replace(">", "")
                    .replace("<", "")
                )
                return "nan".rjust(width)
            except (ValueError, TypeError, IndexError):
                return "nan"
        if self._zero_tol and abs(x) < self._zero_tol:
            x = 0.0
        try:
            return self._fmt(x)
        except (ValueError, TypeError):
            return str(x)


def print_df(
    obj: pd.Series | pd.DataFrame,
    *,
    title: str | None = None,
    index: bool = False,
    max_rows: int | None = None,
    max_cols: int | None = None,
    float_format: str | None = "_.0f",
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    zero_tol: float = 0.0,
    title_color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """Print a Series/DataFrame with optional display controls."""
    if isinstance(obj, pd.Series):
        name = obj.name if obj.name is not None else "value"
        df = obj.to_frame(name=name)
    else:
        df = obj

    if float_format is not None:
        numeric_cols = df.select_dtypes(
            include=["int64", "int32", "float64", "float32"]
        ).columns
        df = df.copy()
        df[numeric_cols] = df[numeric_cols].astype(float)

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    if title:
        df_attrs = f"(shape={df.shape})"
        print_header(
            title=f"DataFrame: {title} {df_attrs}",
            color=title_color,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=False,
        )

    if float_format is None:
        print(df.to_string(index=index, max_rows=max_rows, max_cols=max_cols))
    else:
        formatter = _FloatFormatter(float_format, zero_tol=zero_tol)
        print(
            df.to_string(
                index=index,
                max_rows=max_rows,
                max_cols=max_cols,
                float_format=formatter,
            )
        )

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_dict_of_dfs(
    d: dict[str, pd.DataFrame],
    *,
    title: str | None = None,
    index: bool = False,
    max_rows: int | None = None,
    max_cols: int | None = None,
    float_format: str | None = "_.0f",
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    zero_tol: float = 0.0,
    cols_before_transpose: int = 20,
    title_color: str | None = ANSI_MIDNIGHT_BLUE,
    subtitle_color: str | None = ANSI_BLUE,
) -> None:
    """Print a dictionary of DataFrames with optional display controls."""
    if add_space_before:
        print()

    if title:
        # Use a slightly different color for the main dictionary wrapper if desired,
        # but defaulting to standard title behavior.
        print_header(
            title=f"Dictionary of DataFrames: {title}",
            color=title_color,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=True,
        )

    for key, df in d.items():
        if df.shape[1] > cols_before_transpose:
            df = df.T
            key += " (transposed)"
        print_df(
            df,
            title=key,
            index=index,
            max_rows=max_rows,
            max_cols=max_cols,
            float_format=float_format,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=True,
            zero_tol=zero_tol,
            title_color=subtitle_color,
        )

    if add_space_after:
        print()


def print_array(
    arr: np.ndarray,
    *,
    title: str | None = None,
    max_rows: int | None = None,
    max_cols: int | None = None,
    float_format: str | None = ">10.2f",
    add_space_before: bool = True,
    add_space_after: bool = True,
    zero_tol: float = 0.0,
    title_color: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    """Print a NumPy array with perfectly aligned tabular columns and ANSI colors."""
    _array_info = f"(shape={arr.shape}, dtype={arr.dtype})"

    if add_space_before:
        print()

    if title:
        full_title = f"Array: {title} {_array_info}"
        print_header(
            title=full_title,
            color=title_color,
            add_space_before=False,
            add_space_after=False,
        )

    if float_format is not None and np.issubdtype(arr.dtype, np.integer):
        arr_to_print = arr.astype(float)
    else:
        arr_to_print = arr

    print_opts = {
        "suppress": True,
        "linewidth": 200,
    }

    if max_rows is not None or max_cols is not None:
        r_edge = max_rows // 2 if max_rows is not None else 3
        c_edge = max_cols // 2 if max_cols is not None else 3
        print_opts["edgeitems"] = max(r_edge, c_edge)

        row_limit = max_rows if max_rows is not None else arr_to_print.shape[0]
        col_limit = (
            max_cols
            if max_cols is not None
            else (arr_to_print.shape[1] if arr_to_print.ndim > 1 else 1)
        )
        print_opts["threshold"] = row_limit * col_limit - 1

    if float_format is not None:
        formatter = _FloatFormatter(float_format, zero_tol=zero_tol)
        print_opts["formatter"] = {"float_kind": formatter}

    with np.printoptions(**print_opts):
        print(arr_to_print)

    if add_space_after:
        print()
```

```python
# riskquant/utils/tenors.py
from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import date
from typing import Optional, Tuple


def parse_bracket_token(tenor_token: str) -> Tuple[str, Optional[int]]:
    """
    "[Y-2]" -> ("Y", -2)
    "[M+0]" -> ("M", 0)
    "[D+24]" -> ("D", 24)
    "[HY+1]" -> ("HY", 1)
    "[ON]" -> ("ON", None)   (LABEL)
    """
    # Error.
    if tenor_token is None:
        raise ValueError("tenor_token cannot be None!")

    # Strip white space.
    s = tenor_token.strip()

    # Must start with '[' and end with ']'.
    if s.startswith("[") and s.endswith("]"):
        s = s[1:-1].strip()

    # Find the plus or minus sign.
    plus = s.rfind("+")
    minus = s.rfind("-")
    sign_pos = max(plus, minus)

    # If no sign.
    if sign_pos <= 0:
        return s, None

    # Unit (Y, M, D, HY, etc.)
    unit = s[:sign_pos]

    # Signed offset.
    offset = int(s[sign_pos:])

    # Return.
    return unit, offset


def get_spot_year(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    roll_month: int,
    roll_day: int,
    post_roll_shift: int,
) -> int:
    """
    Defines which delivery year corresponds to Y+0 under ROLL_ON_MMDD.
    """

    # Get the roll date,
    roll_date = date(as_of_date.year, roll_month, roll_day)

    # Compare to as-of-date.
    # As-of-date is on or after the roll date.
    if as_of_date >= roll_date:
        return (
            as_of_date.year + post_roll_shift
        )  # Current year plus the post_roll_shift amount (in years).

    # As-of-date is before the roll date.
    # Previous year plus the post_roll_shift amount (in years).
    return (as_of_date.year - 1) + post_roll_shift


def add_months_to_date_and_return_yyyymm01(
    *,
    d: date,
    months_to_add: int,
) -> date:
    """Add months to a month-start date (day=1)."""
    y = d.year + (d.month - 1 + months_to_add) // 12
    m = (d.month - 1 + months_to_add) % 12 + 1
    return date(y, m, 1)


def fmt_date_as_str_yyyy(d: date) -> str:
    return f"{d.year:04d}"


def fmt_date_as_str_yyyymm(d: date) -> str:
    return f"{d.year:04d}{d.month:02d}"


def fmt_date_as_str_yyyymmdd(d: date) -> str:
    return f"{d.year:04d}{d.month:02d}{d.day:02d}"
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\valuation\engine.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/engine.py

"""
Portfolio valuation engine.

Owns the instrument-id -> evaluator dispatch table and the scenario valuation
loop. Evaluator resolution lives here, in the valuation layer: `run_valuation`
reads each block's `INSTRUMENT_ID` class constant and indexes `BLOCK_EVALUATORS`
directly. `CompiledPortfolio` carries no evaluator references, so the dependency
runs one way only — `valuation -> portfolio` — with no import cycle.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Final, Protocol, TypeVar, cast, runtime_checkable

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.market_data.state import MarketDataMatrix
from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.base_block import Block
from riskquant.portfolio.compiled import CompiledPortfolio
from riskquant.portfolio.instruments import (
    NUM_INSTRUMENTS,
    SPOT_ID,
    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE_ID,
    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE_ID,
    VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE_ID,
    VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE_ID,
    Instrument,
)

# VALUATORS
from riskquant.valuation.pricers.spot import price_spot_block
from riskquant.valuation.pricers.vanilla_european_option_curve import (
    price_vanilla_european_option_curve_block,
)
from riskquant.valuation.pricers.vanilla_european_option_node import (
    price_vanilla_european_option_node_block,
)
from riskquant.valuation.pricers.vanilla_forward_curve import (
    price_vanilla_forward_curve_block,
)
from riskquant.valuation.pricers.vanilla_forward_node import (
    price_vanilla_forward_node_block,
)
from riskquant.valuation.result import ValuationResult

# ==============================================================================
# EVALUATOR PROTOCOL
# ==============================================================================

# 1. Define a contravariant TypeVar bound to Block
B_contra = TypeVar("B_contra", bound=Block, contravariant=True)


@runtime_checkable
class Evaluator(Protocol[B_contra]):
    """
    Structural contract for all block evaluators (pricers + risk evaluators).
    """

    def __call__(
        self,
        *,
        block: B_contra,  # 2. Use the TypeVar instead of base Block
        market_data_mtx: MarketDataMatrix,
        out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    ) -> None: ...


# ==============================================================================
# DISPATCH TABLE
# ==============================================================================

# 3. Explicitly type the list elements as Evaluator[Any] | None
_BLOCK_EVALUATORS_MUT: list[Evaluator[Any] | None] = [None] * NUM_INSTRUMENTS


def _register(instrument_id: int, evaluator: Evaluator[Any]) -> None:
    # runtime_checkable ignores TypeVars, so this check still works natively
    if not isinstance(evaluator, Evaluator):
        raise TypeError(
            f"Evaluator for instrument_id={instrument_id} does not satisfy the "
            f"Evaluator protocol: {evaluator!r}"
        )
    _BLOCK_EVALUATORS_MUT[instrument_id] = evaluator


_register(
    int(SPOT_ID),
    price_spot_block,
)
_register(
    int(VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE_ID),
    price_vanilla_forward_node_block,
)
_register(
    int(VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE_ID),
    price_vanilla_forward_curve_block,
)
_register(
    int(VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE_ID),
    price_vanilla_european_option_node_block,
)
_register(
    int(VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE_ID),
    price_vanilla_european_option_curve_block,
)


# Freeze. No mutations after module load.
# This tells the type checker: "Trust me, the runtime guard above handled the Nones."
BLOCK_EVALUATORS: Final[tuple[Evaluator[Any], ...]] = cast(
    tuple[Evaluator[Any], ...], tuple(_BLOCK_EVALUATORS_MUT)
)

# Hard fail at import if any instrument has no evaluator: catches the case where
# a new Instrument variant is added but not wired up here.
_unregistered = [
    Instrument(i).name for i, ev in enumerate(BLOCK_EVALUATORS) if ev is None
]
if _unregistered:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"No evaluator registered for instruments: {_unregistered}. "
        f"Register them in engine.py."
    )


def resolve_evaluator(block: Block) -> Evaluator:
    """
    Resolve the evaluator for a compiled block via its `INSTRUMENT_ID`.

    Single-array index — no `type()`, no isinstance ladder, no string keys.
    """
    evaluator = BLOCK_EVALUATORS[block.INSTRUMENT_ID]
    if evaluator is None:  # unreachable given the import-time guard above
        raise RuntimeError(
            f"No evaluator for INSTRUMENT_ID={block.INSTRUMENT_ID} "
            f"({type(block).__name__})."
        )
    return evaluator


# ==============================================================================
# VALUATION ENGINE
# ==============================================================================


def run_valuation(
    *,
    portfolio: CompiledPortfolio,
    market_data_mtx: MarketDataMatrix,
) -> ValuationResult:
    """
    Execute full portfolio valuation across all scenarios.

    Iterates pre-compiled blocks in portfolio order, resolves each block's
    evaluator from its `INSTRUMENT_ID`, and dispatches it. All writes target
    contiguous slices of a single pre-allocated (n_trades x n_scenarios) C-order
    output array. Zero intermediate allocations; the GIL is bypassed inside the
    Numba kernels.

    Returns
    -------
    npt.NDArray[np.float64]
        Shape (n_trades, n_scenarios), C-contiguous. Row i corresponds to the
        trade at portfolio offset i.
    """
    n_trades: int = portfolio.total_trades
    n_scenarios: int = market_data_mtx.num_scenarios

    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = np.empty(
        (n_trades, n_scenarios),
        dtype=np.float64,
        order="C",
    )

    # Hoist repeated attribute lookups out of the loop.
    blocks = portfolio.blocks
    offsets = portfolio.block_offsets
    evaluators_table = BLOCK_EVALUATORS
    md = market_data_mtx

    for i in range(portfolio.total_blocks):
        block = blocks[i]
        start = int(offsets[i])
        end = start + block.num_trades

        # id -> evaluator: one O(1) table index, resolved here in the engine.
        evaluator = evaluators_table[block.INSTRUMENT_ID]

        evaluator(
            block=block,
            market_data_mtx=md,
            out=out[start:end, :],
        )

    return ValuationResult(
        trade_uids=portfolio.trade_uids,
        values=out,
    )
```

```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricers/spot.py
"""
Spot pricer.

PV = qty * spot_price * fx_effective

`fx_spots_effective` rows are already the resolved transform (direct or
pre-inverted) — pricer kernels do no inversion math. Per-trade FX is one
gather + one multiply per scenario. The `fx_effective_uix < 0` sentinel
selects the "no FX" branch which is hoisted outside the scenario loop.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.valuation.kernels.spot import _kernel_price_spot

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.market_data.state import MarketDataMatrix
    from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.spot_block import SpotBlock


def price_spot_block(
    *,
    block: SpotBlock,
    market_data_mtx: MarketDataMatrix,
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """Spot pricer entrypoint. All arrays must be C-contiguous and dtype-stable."""
    _kernel_price_spot(
        out,
        market_data_mtx.prices,
        block.price_uix,
        block.qty,
        market_data_mtx.fx_spots_effective,
        block.fx_spots_effective_uix,
    )
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\valuation\pricers\vanilla_european_option_curve.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricers/vanilla_european_option_curve.py
"""
Vanilla European option pricer, curve variant.

Identical CIP-forward + Black-76 math to the node variant, with two
structural differences:

  - The underlying scalar comes from `price_curve_grid[curve_uix, delivery_uix, s]`.
  - Volatility is sourced from a 4D vol surface and requires per-scenario
    strike-axis interpolation: `np.interp` over the y-axis pillars.

The y-axis-type id determines the strike coordinate used for the
interpolation (strike, log-strike, moneyness vs forward, log-moneyness, ...).

FX rows are already EFFECTIVE — no inversion math; the strike-FX rate is
loaded once per scenario, used as a divisor for CIP forward synthesis and
as a multiplier for the PV translation back to settle ccy.

Mock-vol fallback
-----------------
When `MarketDataMatrix.vol_surfaces` is empty (e.g., calibration not yet
landed), a flat `FALLBACK_MOCK_VOLATILITY_DO_NOT_SHIP` is used. This is a
development convenience and MUST not ship in production reports.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.constants import (
    NOT_IMPLEMENTED_SENTINEL,
)
from riskquant.valuation.kernels.vanilla_european_option_curve import (
    _kernel_price_vanilla_european_option_curve,
)

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.market_data.state import MarketDataMatrix
    from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.vanilla_european_option_block import (
        VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock,
    )


def price_vanilla_european_option_curve_block(
    *,
    block: VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock,
    market_data_mtx: MarketDataMatrix,
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """European-option-curve pricer entrypoint."""
    if np.any(block.vol_surface_y_axis_type_id == np.int8(NOT_IMPLEMENTED_SENTINEL)):
        raise NotImplementedError(
            "vol_surface_y_axis_type_id contains unresolved sentinel values. "
            "CompiledIngestSoA must supply vol_surface_y_axis_type_ids before "
            "vanilla European option curve trades can be priced."
        )

    _kernel_price_vanilla_european_option_curve(
        out,
        market_data_mtx.price_curve_grid,
        market_data_mtx.vol_surface_strikes,
        market_data_mtx.vol_surfaces,
        market_data_mtx.fx_spots_effective,
        market_data_mtx.discount_factor_grid,
        market_data_mtx.tte_grid,
        block.qty,
        block.strike,
        block.cp_mult,
        block.underlying_price_curve_uix,
        block.vol_surface_uix,
        block.vol_surface_y_axis_type_id,
        block.fx_under_to_settle_effective_uix,
        block.under_discount_curve_uix,
        block.fx_strike_to_settle_effective_uix,
        block.strike_discount_curve_uix,
        block.delivery_date_uix,
        block.expiry_date_uix,
        block.settlement_date_uix,
    )
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\valuation\pricers\vanilla_european_option_node.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricers/vanilla_european_option_node.py
"""
Vanilla European option pricer, node variant.

Synthesises the forward in strike currency via Covered Interest Parity,
prices via Black-76, then discounts and FX-translates back to settle ccy.

Math (per trade i, scenario s)
------------------------------
    rate_k = fx_spots_effective[fx_k_eff_uix(i), s]   # k_ccy -> settle_ccy

    # CIP forward in strike currency
    fwd_strike_ccy
        = forward_node[p, s]
          * fx_spots_effective[fx_u_eff_uix(i), s]    # u_ccy -> settle_ccy
          * df_grid[df_u_uix(i), expiry_uix(i), s]    # under-DF at expiry
          / rate_k                                    # settle_ccy -> k_ccy
          / df_grid[df_k_uix(i), expiry_uix(i), s]    # strike-DF at expiry

    # Black-76 future value (cp_mult = +1 call, -1 put)
    sigma = vol_nodes[v, s]
    std_dev = max(sigma * sqrt(tte), eps)
    d1 = (log(fwd / k) + 0.5 * sigma**2 * tte) / std_dev
    d2 = d1 - std_dev
    fv = cp * (fwd * N(cp * d1) - k * N(cp * d2))

    # PV discount in strike ccy, then translate back to settle ccy
    pv_strike_ccy = fv * df_grid[df_k_uix(i), settle_uix(i), s]
    pv_settle_ccy = pv_strike_ccy * rate_k

    out[i, s] = qty[i] * pv_settle_ccy

FX rows are already EFFECTIVE in `MarketDataMatrix.fx_spots_effective`; the
kernel does NO inversion math. The strike-FX rate `rate_k` is loaded once
per scenario and used as a divisor in the forward synthesis and as a
multiplier in the PV translation — equivalent counts to the legacy code
but with the `(1.0 / rate_k) if inv_k else rate_k` gymnastics gone.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.valuation.kernels.vanilla_european_option_node import (
    _kernel_price_vanilla_european_option_node,
)

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.market_data.state import MarketDataMatrix
    from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.vanilla_european_option_block import (
        VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock,
    )


def price_vanilla_european_option_node_block(
    *,
    block: VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock,
    market_data_mtx: MarketDataMatrix,
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """European-option-node pricer entrypoint."""
    _kernel_price_vanilla_european_option_node(
        out,
        market_data_mtx.forward_nodes,
        market_data_mtx.vol_nodes,
        market_data_mtx.fx_spots_effective,
        market_data_mtx.discount_factor_grid,
        market_data_mtx.sqrt_tte_grid,
        market_data_mtx.half_tte_grid,
        block.qty,
        block.strike,
        block.cp_mult,
        block.underlying_price_uix,
        block.vol_uix,
        block.fx_under_to_settle_effective_uix,
        block.under_discount_curve_uix,
        block.fx_strike_to_settle_effective_uix,
        block.strike_discount_curve_uix,
        block.expiry_date_uix,
        block.settlement_date_uix,
    )
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricers/vanilla_forward_curve.py
"""
Vanilla forward pricer, curve variant.

Identical math to the node variant; the only structural change is that the
underlying scalar comes from `price_curve_grid[curve_uix, delivery_uix, s]`
instead of `forward_nodes[uix, s]`.

FX rows are already EFFECTIVE in `MarketDataMatrix.fx_spots_effective`;
no inversion math survives into this kernel.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.valuation.kernels.vanilla_forward_curve import (
    _kernel_price_vanilla_forward_curve,
)

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.market_data.state import MarketDataMatrix
    from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.vanilla_forward_block import (
        VanillaForwardCurveBlock,
    )


def price_vanilla_forward_curve_block(
    *,
    block: VanillaForwardCurveBlock,
    market_data_mtx: MarketDataMatrix,
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """Forward-curve pricer entrypoint."""
    _kernel_price_vanilla_forward_curve(
        out,
        market_data_mtx.price_curve_grid,
        market_data_mtx.fx_spots_effective,
        market_data_mtx.discount_factor_grid,
        block.qty,
        block.strike,
        block.price_curve_uix,
        block.delivery_date_uix,
        block.fx_under_to_settle_effective_uix,
        block.under_discount_curve_uix,
        block.fx_strike_to_settle_effective_uix,
        block.strike_discount_curve_uix,
        block.settlement_date_uix,
    )
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\valuation\pricers\vanilla_forward_node.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricers/vanilla_forward_node.py
"""
Vanilla forward pricer, node variant.

    leg_u = forward_node * fx_u_effective * df_u(stl)
    leg_k = strike       * fx_k_effective * df_k(stl)
    PV    = qty * (leg_u - leg_k)

FX rows are already EFFECTIVE (direct or pre-inverted) inside
`MarketDataMatrix.fx_spots_effective` — pricer kernels do NO inversion math.
Each FX gather is one indexed read + one multiply per scenario.

Optional legs (FX / DF) are signalled by `INVALID_UIX (< 0)` in the
corresponding `_uix` field. These predicates are trade-level invariants;
Numba's LICM hoists them outside the inner scenario loop, so the inner loop
streams straight-line arithmetic over C-contiguous memory.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.valuation.kernels.vanilla_forward_node import (
    _kernel_price_vanilla_forward_node,
)

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.market_data.state import MarketDataMatrix
    from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.vanilla_forward_block import (
        VanillaForwardNodeBlock,
    )


def price_vanilla_forward_node_block(
    *,
    block: VanillaForwardNodeBlock,
    market_data_mtx: MarketDataMatrix,
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """Forward-node pricer entrypoint."""
    _kernel_price_vanilla_forward_node(
        out,
        market_data_mtx.forward_nodes,
        market_data_mtx.fx_spots_effective,
        market_data_mtx.discount_factor_grid,
        block.qty,
        block.strike,
        block.price_uix,
        block.fx_under_to_settle_effective_uix,
        block.under_discount_curve_uix,
        block.fx_strike_to_settle_effective_uix,
        block.strike_discount_curve_uix,
        block.settlement_date_uix,
    )
```
</file>
